  [G7.2] Initial and ongoing adviser fees, consistent with the client agreement
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   Initial advice fee: 3% of amount invested, stated monetary equivalent £9,838.36 (with current transfer values, facilitated from plan). Ongoing service charge: 1% per annum of fund value, stated monetary equivalent circa £3,181.08 for first year (based on current values and expected contributions, minus the initial fee, facilitated from plan).
    File:     Client Agreement [P6] states: 'Initial Service - There will be a charge of 3% of the value of the funds that we advise on with a minimum of £1,350 fee. Based on the value of your funds, our fee will be £9,839.36'. Client Agreement also states: 'Ongoing Services - For most investment funds that we transact on your behalf, we charge an ongoing annual review management charge of 1% (subject to a minimum fee of £100), which is automatically taken from the funds invested.' The percentage rates (3% initial, 1% ongoing) match the report. The monetary initial fee in the Client Agreement is £9,839.36, whereas the report states £9,838.36 — a £1.00 difference. The basis for both the £9,838/9,839 figures is not explicitly stated in the Client Agreement as a combined fund including the Pension Sharing Order (PSO) of £260,000 plus True Potential £46,672.80 plus Nest £21,272.64 = ~£327,945; however 3% of £327,945 = ~£9,838, which reconciles. The stated transfer value in the report's recommended solution table is £67,945.44 (True Potential + Nest only); 3% of £67,945.44 = £2,038.36, which does not match £9,838.36. The report never explicitly states the basis of the £9,838.36 figure as including the PSO funds.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states initial fee monetary amount as £9,838.36; Client Agreement states £9,839.36 — a £1.00 discrepancy.
      - Report states initial fee as 3% of 'amount invested'; stated transfer value of recommended solution is £67,945.44, and 3% of that equals £2,038.36, not £9,838.36. The basis for the £9,838.36 figure is never explicitly reconciled in the report (the PSO of £260,000 must be included to reach ~£327,945 and 3% thereof ~£9,838, but this is not stated).
      - Ongoing fee monetary equivalent of £3,181.08 is stated as based on 'current values and expected contributions, minus the initial fee' but the basis is not explicitly shown: 1% of £67,945.44 = £679.45 (does not match); 1% of (~£327,945 − £9,838) = ~£3,181 (matches only if PSO funds are included and initial fee deducted, but this basis is never stated in the report).
      - The Aviva charges document [P8] confirms an initial adviser charge is to be deducted, but the specific monetary amount agreed in that document is not legible from the retrieved passages — the figure cannot be independently verified from the illustration.
    The percentage rates are consistent: both the report and the Client Agreement [P6] state 3% initial and 1% ongoing, so the rates themselves match. The £1.00 difference between the report's £9,838.36 and the Client Agreement's £9,839.36 is a minor rounding discrepancy that is unlikely to be material but is nonetheless a factual mismatch between two documents. The more significant issue is the report's failure to state clearly what the £9,838.36 is 3% of. The report's own recommended solution table shows a total transfer value of £67,945.44 (True Potential + Nest), and 3% of that equals £2,038.36 — a figure nearly £7,800 lower than the stated fee. The derived figures confirm the only way to reach ~£9,838 is to apply 3% to the combined total of all three arrangements including the Quilter PSO (£260,000 + £46,672.80 + £21,272.64 = £327,945.44; 3% = £9,838.36). However, the report never states this as the basis, and the PSO is a pension sharing order receipt rather than a straightforward transfer being arranged, raising a question about whether a 3% initial charge is appropriate on that element and whether it falls within the 'amount invested' basis described. The same absence of stated basis applies to the ongoing fee: £3,181.08 is consistent only with 1% applied to the total combined fund less the initial fee (~£318,107), but this is never explained. The Client Agreement [P6] confirms a minimum fee of £1,350 is applicable, so the fee of £9,838/£9,839 clears that threshold comfortably. The ongoing 1% minimum of £100 is also clearly met. No vulnerability indicators in the file are identified as bearing on this specific arithmetic question. The core finding is a disclosure shortfall: the report states a monetary initial fee that cannot be reconciled with the only fund total it explicitly states (£67,945.44), and never provides the reconciliation basis. This is compounded by a £1.00 discrepancy between the report and the Client Agreement. The comparison on rates is clean; the comparison on monetary amounts is unsupported as to basis and contains a minor numerical discrepancy.

  [G7.3] Cumulative impact of costs and charges
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report's Critical Yield appendix states three scenario rows for the recommended Aviva solution: (Low/0% growth) RIY 1.5%, critical yield 0.41% (+0.41%), effect of switching -12.42%; (Mid/2.94% growth) RIY 1.5%, critical yield 3.37% (+0.43%), effect of switching -12.42%; (High/5.88% growth) RIY 1.6%, critical yield 6.32% (+0.44%), effect of switching -12.42%. The existing schemes are valued at £48,408 / £121,807 / £298,632 and the Aviva Pension Portfolio at £42,300 / £106,000 / £261,000 across the three growth scenarios. These figures are described as real-terms (inflation-adjusted). The basis stated is: initial adviser charge 3% taken from the new solution; ongoing annual adviser charge 1.00%; existing advice charges on True Potential also included. The report notes the initial advice fee of 3% produces a monetary figure of £9,838.36.
    File:     Passage [P19] (the report itself, category I) reproduces the critical yield table verbatim. The switching analysis document [P22] (category G, 'Annes M SAP IAC only ex existing OAC 24.3.26') shows Aviva projected values of £58,200 / £146,000 / £359,000 with an RIY of 0.5%, and existing plan values of £53,497.38 / £134,612.81 / £330,027.33 at the same growth rates, all at retirement age 87. Passage [P23] (category G, 'Annes M SAP no charges ex existing OAC 24.3.26') shows the same existing plan figures (True Potential £34,162.52 / £85,961.45 / £210,749.84 at age 87 and Nest £21,272.64 current value) consistent with the switching analysis. Passage [P24] (category G) confirms the Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) projected values of £58,200 / £146,000 / £359,000 with RIY 0.5% and existing plan £53,497.38 / £134,612.81 / £330,027.33. Passage [P21] (category H, Aviva illustration) shows the Aviva platform charge at 0.19% and fund OCF/weighted average at 0.20%, consistent with the 0.39% annual recurring charge in the report. The Aviva illustration and switching analysis documents therefore sit alongside the report's critical yield section but show materially different figures.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report's critical yield table states Aviva projected values of £42,300 / £106,000 / £261,000, while the switching analysis documents [P22]/[P24] state £58,200 / £146,000 / £359,000 for the same three growth scenarios — differences of approximately £15,900, £40,000 and £98,000 respectively.
      - The report's critical yield table states existing scheme values of £48,408 / £121,807 / £298,632, while the switching analysis [P22]/[P24] states existing plan values of £53,497.38 / £134,612.81 / £330,027.33 — differences of approximately £5,089, £12,806 and £31,395 respectively.
      - The report's RIY is stated as 1.5% (Low/Mid) and 1.6% (High), whereas the switching analysis [P24] shows an RIY of 0.5% — a difference of 1.0–1.1 percentage points.
      - The effect of switching is stated as -12.42% in the report, but calculating from the switching analysis figures: at Low growth (£42,300 vs £48,408) the implied effect is approximately -12.6%, broadly consistent, but at Mid growth (£106,000 vs £121,807) = -13.0% and at High growth (£261,000 vs £298,632) = -12.6%, which are not exactly -12.42% in every scenario either.
      - The report's critical yield table states the rate of return required from Aviva as 0.41%, 3.37% and 6.32%, but these cannot be verified against the switching analysis documents because the latter do not state a critical yield or rate-of-return-required figure.
      - The monetary initial advice fee of £9,838.36 is not arithmetically reconcilable with 3% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 (which would give £2,038.36), and the basis for the larger figure (3% of ~£327,945 including the PSO funds) is not explicitly stated anywhere in the critical yield section or the switching analysis documents.
      - The ongoing monetary charge is stated as circa £3,181.08 for the first year at 1%, which is consistent with 1% of ~£318,108 (i.e. combined fund net of initial fee), but this basis is not stated explicitly; 1% of the disclosed transfer value of £67,945.44 would give only £679.45.
    The central finding is a material inconsistency between the projected values and RIY figures presented in the report's Critical Yield appendix and those in the switching analysis documents in the case file. The report states Aviva projected values of £42,300 / £106,000 / £261,000 and an RIY of 1.5%/1.6%, while the switching analysis documents [P22] and [P24] — which are category G (non-report) evidence — state £58,200 / £146,000 / £359,000 and an RIY of 0.5%. The projected values in the documents are approximately 37–38% higher than those in the report's critical yield table at every growth rate. The RIY shown in the documents (0.5%) is 1.0–1.1 percentage points lower than the report asserts (1.5%/1.6%). These are not rounding differences; they are material divergences that suggest the critical yield comparison in the report was run on a different basis or a different illustration from the one in the pack. A plausible explanation is that the report's critical yield table was produced from a separate switching analysis tool (e.g. one that included the initial advice charge and deducted it immediately, reducing the starting Aviva fund value substantially), while [P22]/[P24] appear to be a run with initial adviser charge only (document name 'SAP IAC only ex existing OAC'). The document [P23] appears to be a run with no charges at all ('SAP no charges ex existing OAC'). Neither document matches the report's projected values or its stated RIY of 1.5%/1.6%. Because no document in the pack corresponds to the report's critical yield figures, those figures are unsupported by the case file evidence. The report itself (passages [P19], all category I) cannot provide corroboration. Additionally, the initial advice fee arithmetic is unresolved: the report states £9,838.36 at 3%, but 3% of the two transfer values (£67,945.44) gives £2,038.36 — only consistent if the PSO funds (~£260,000) are included, which is never stated as the basis in the critical yield section. This means the basis on which the 3% charge was deducted from the critical yield illustration is unclear, further undermining confidence in the projected figures. On vulnerability: the file records the client as recently divorced and transitioning to self-employment. No specific vulnerability flags are recorded that would separately affect this cost-and-charges assessment beyond the general need for clear, accurate disclosures applicable to all clients. The discrepancy in projected values and RIY is the dominant finding.

  [G7.4] Cost comparison with alternative options
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The Pension Switch Charges Only Assessment table compares existing True Potential (EA2) at 0.98% / £457.39 per annum against the recommended Aviva solution at 0.39%, showing a saving of -0.59% / -£275.37. For Nest (EA3) the existing charge is 0.30% / £63.82, with the recommended solution costing +0.09% / +£19.15 more. The comparison is stated to be on a 'like for like' basis excluding ongoing adviser charges. The existing arrangements section separately discloses True Potential charges as: platform 0.40%, ongoing advice 0.50%, weighted average fund management 0.58% — summing to 1.48%. The switch table uses 0.98% for True Potential, which excludes the 0.50% ongoing advice charge per the like-for-like methodology. However 0.40% + 0.58% = 0.98%, which is consistent excluding advice. The report also states the recommended Aviva total recurring charge is 0.39% (platform 0.19% + fund 0.20%) applied to the combined fund value of £327,945.44 (all three arrangements including Quilter PSO of £260,000). The Quilter PSO (EA1) is absent from the switch charges comparison table entirely.
    File:     [P4] (suitability report, category I) reproduces the switch charges table showing True Potential 0.98% / £457.39 / -0.59% / -£275.37 and Nest 0.30% / £63.82 / +0.09% / +£19.15. [P16] (suitability report, category I) states True Potential platform charges 0.40%, ongoing advice 0.50%, weighted average fund management 0.58%. [P23] (G — SelectaPension SAP illustration) shows True Potential fund value at valuation date £46,672.80 and Nest fund value not fully quoted in retrieved text but implied by the charges arithmetic. [P6] (G — SelectaPension SAP All charges ex existing OAC) shows the existing plan projected values. [P5] (suitability report, I) shows the critical yield appendix referencing the same existing/new plan values. No provider document (True Potential or Nest product literature) independently states the 0.98% or 0.30% total recurring charge figures used in the switch table. The True Potential KIIDs in file ([P12], [P15], [P18], [P22]) quote fund OCFs of 0.76%, 0.63%, 0.87%, 0.54% respectively — none of these individually matches the 0.58% weighted average fund charge stated in the report, and no weighted average calculation is shown. No Nest product document in the pack independently states the 0.30% plan charge used in the switch table.
    Discrepancies:
      - 1. True Potential total recurring charge in the switch table is 0.98%, but the existing arrangements table lists platform 0.40% + ongoing advice 0.50% + fund management 0.58% = 1.48%. The switch table excludes the 0.50% ongoing advice charge to reach 0.98% (0.40% + 0.58%), but this basis is not consistently labelled; the existing arrangements table presents all three charges without distinguishing which are excluded from the like-for-like basis.
      - 2. The 0.58% weighted average fund management charge for True Potential is asserted in the report but no weighted average calculation is shown. The individual True Potential fund KIIDs in the pack show OCFs of 0.87% (Goldman Sachs Balanced), 0.54% (Growth Aligned Aggressive), 0.63% (UBS Aggressive), 0.76% (Allianz Balanced) — none of which on its own equals 0.58%, and no blended calculation is evidenced in any retrieved passage.
      - 3. The Quilter PSO (EA1) is included in the recommended solution (and the Aviva annual recurring charge of 0.39% / £1,278.99 is applied to the full £327,945.44 fund), but EA1 is entirely absent from the switch charges comparison table. The like-for-like cost comparison therefore covers only two of the three arrangements being switched, omitting the largest single component (£260,000 / ~79% of the total fund value).
      - 4. The monetary impact figures in the switch table (-£275.37 for True Potential saving; +£19.15 additional cost for Nest) are computed on individual existing fund values. The net combined saving is not stated, so the reader cannot see the aggregate charge impact across all three arrangements.
      - 5. EA2 (True Potential) charge arithmetic: 0.98% × £46,672.80 = £457.39. This is confirmed by the derived figures and corroborated by [P4] and [P23]. No discrepancy here, but the basis (excluding ongoing advice) is not explicitly stated in the switch table itself.
      - 6. EA3 (Nest) charge arithmetic: 0.30% × £21,272.64 = £63.82 (derived figures confirm). The recommended impact: Aviva 0.39% on Nest's value = £82.96; difference = +£19.14 (rounds to £19.15 per the report). Arithmetically consistent. No discrepancy on the numbers alone, but the 0.30% Nest charge is not independently corroborated by any Nest product document in the pack.
      - 7. No provider document in the pack independently corroborates the 0.98% True Potential total recurring charge or the 0.30% Nest plan charge as used in the switch table — both figures appear only in the suitability report itself (category I passages).
    Several issues compound here. First, the most serious is the complete omission of EA1 (the Quilter PSO, £260,000 — approximately 79% of the total fund value being consolidated) from the switch charges comparison table. The report discloses a conditional Quilter retention platform charge of 0.27% and justifies the switch on cost grounds (saving of £208 per annum vs 0.19% Aviva negotiated rate), but this comparison never appears in the formal switch charges table. The like-for-like cost comparison therefore covers only £67,945.44 of the £327,945.44 being moved, while the Aviva ongoing charge of 0.39% / £1,278.99 is calculated on the full combined fund. This asymmetry means the switch table understates both the existing total cost (by omitting Quilter's charges on £260,000) and the new total cost (which the annual recurring charges table does show on the full fund). The reader cannot derive the true aggregate charge comparison from the information provided. Second, the True Potential 0.98% total in the switch table is the like-for-like figure (excluding the 0.50% ongoing advice charge), but this basis is not labelled in the switch table itself — it appears only in the narrative. The existing arrangements section lists all three charge layers including the 0.50% advice charge without flagging which is excluded for like-for-like purposes. The extraction's recorded internal inconsistency confirms this: 0.40% + 0.58% = 0.98% (consistent with the switch table only if advice is excluded), yet the existing arrangements section presents 1.48% in total across all three lines. The guard about comparisons on different bases applies: the 0.98% and the 1.48% are on different bases (with and without ongoing advice), so they are not two contradictory answers to one question. However, the failure to label the basis in the switch table is a disclosure shortfall. Third, the 0.58% weighted average fund management charge for True Potential is unverified. The four True Potential fund KIIDs in the pack show OCFs ranging from 0.54% to 0.87%; their fund-value-weighted average cannot be computed without knowing the split across all six funds held (only five are listed with values in the existing arrangements table: Goldman Sachs Balanced £2,787.29, Growth Aligned Aggressive £10,764.88, Schroders Balanced £2,378.80, SEI Aggressive £7,254.00, Trinity Bridge Balanced £7,771.29, UBS Aggressive £10,099.00 — six funds totalling £46,672.26 but OCFs for Schroders Balanced, SEI Aggressive and Trinity Bridge Balanced are not in the pack). No provider document in the case file independently confirms the 0.98% total or 0.58% component; both rest solely on report assertions (I-category passages). This is the 'evidence absent' scenario described in the check logic. Fourth, the ongoing adviser charge situation on the recommended Aviva plan is the 1% p.a. / approximately £3,181 per year — this is disclosed in the report but absent from the switch charges table (which is explicitly like-for-like excluding advice on both sides). The critical yield appendix [P5] does include adviser charges and shows the Aviva plan is more expensive than True Potential when adviser fees are included, with a required outperformance of only 0.01% p.a. — a modest hurdle. For Nest, the required outperformance rises to 1.23% p.a., which is disclosed in [P10]. The derived figures note that 1.23% × £1,556.91 = £19.15, implying the required outperformance calculation is applied to a very small residual rather than the full Nest fund value, which is unexplained and potentially misleading — though this ambiguity is within the report itself and [P5] provides the fuller critical yield context. On vulnerability: no vulnerability indicators are identified in the pack that would materially affect this cost-and-charges assessment beyond what is noted above. The aggregate of findings is: (a) the largest arrangement is absent from the comparison table with no stated reason in the table; (b) the True Potential weighted average fund charge is unverified by any provider document; (c) no net combined charge impact is presented; (d) the basis for the like-for-like exclusion of adviser fees is unlabelled in the table. These together constitute material gaps in the cost comparison disclosure.

  [G7.5] Benefit versus cost — fair value
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The report asserts the following benefit/cost value claims: (1) Quilter platform charge 0.27% vs Aviva negotiated 0.19%, saving £208 p.a. (2) True Potential platform charge 0.40% vs Aviva 0.21%, saving 0.19% or £88.68 p.a. (3) True Potential fund charge 0.58% vs new plan 0.20%, saving £177.35 p.a. (4) Nest: new plan 0.39% vs existing 0.30%, small increase of £19.15 p.a. (5) Initial advice fee 3% = £9,838.36. (6) Ongoing service 1% p.a. = circa £3,181.08 for first year. (7) Qualitative benefits: investment flexibility, online platform access, flexible retirement options, ongoing monitoring. (8) The like-for-like switch analysis shows saving of 0.59% for True Potential (existing 0.98% vs new 0.39%). (9) The report does not state the basis for the £9,838.36 initial fee, nor the combined fund value (c.£327,945) on which both the initial fee and ongoing fee appear to be calculated.
    File:     The Selectapension switch analyses (P8, P9, P10, P11, P12, P14, P15) are in the case file. P11 (SAP All charges ex existing OAC) shows Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) AMC of 0.25% pa, reduced by 0.08% decrease, with fund charges of 0.2100% (HSBC) and 0.2207% (Vanguard), weighted average 0.2154%. P14 (SAP no charges ex existing OAC) shows projected inflation-adjusted values: Aviva £60,100/£151,000/£370,000 vs Existing £53,497/£134,613/£330,027 at 0%/2.94%/5.88% growth, RIY 0.4% for Aviva. P12/P15 (IAC only ex existing OAC) show True Potential transfer value £46,672.80 and Nest values. P10/P17 (All charges inc existing OAC) and P8/P9 provide fund performance data. The SAP analyses do not contain a figure for the Quilter/PSO platform charge of 0.27% to cross-reference the £208 saving. No provider illustration from Aviva is present in the retrieved passages that states negotiated platform rate of 0.19%. The derived figures confirm: 0.19% of £327,947 = £623.10 (consistent with negotiated rate applying to full combined fund); 0.21% of True Potential-only fund would be £97.89, not consistent with how the 0.21% is described; the True Potential fund saving of £88.68 at 0.19% of £46,672.80 = £88.68 (consistent). Nest increase of £19.15 at 0.09% of £21,272.64 = £19.15 (consistent). NP1 total ongoing 0.39% of £327,946 = £1,278.99. Ongoing 1% of (£327,945 − £9,838) ≈ £3,181 (broadly consistent if PSO included and initial fee deducted). The report states Aviva platform charge as both 0.19% (negotiated, vs Quilter 0.27%) and 0.21% (vs True Potential 0.40%) — these are two different figures for the same platform used in the same recommendation, without explanation. The SAP (P11) states AMC of 0.25% reduced by 0.08% = 0.17% net AMC, not 0.19% or 0.21%. The initial fee of £9,838.36 at 3% is arithmetically irreconcilable with the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 (which gives £2,038.36); it is consistent only with 3% of the full combined fund of ~£327,945 (including the Quilter PSO of £260,000), but the report never states this basis. No Aviva provider illustration is present in the evidence to confirm charges, projections or the negotiated rate.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report states the Aviva platform charge as 0.19% (when comparing against Quilter) and also as 0.21% (when comparing against True Potential) — two different figures for the same platform charge in the same recommendation, with no explanation of the difference.
      - The SAP analysis (P11) states the Aviva Pension Portfolio AMC as 0.25% reduced by a decrease of 0.08%, implying a net AMC of 0.17%, which matches neither the 0.19% nor the 0.21% figures stated in the report.
      - The initial advice fee of £9,838.36 is stated as 3% of the amount invested, but 3% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 = £2,038.36, not £9,838.36. The fee is consistent with 3% of ~£327,945 (full combined fund including PSO), but this basis is never stated in the report.
      - The ongoing fee of circa £3,181.08 at 1% p.a. is consistent with 1% of the combined fund net of initial fee (~£318,107), but 1% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 = only £679.45. The basis (full combined fund including PSO) is not stated.
      - No Aviva provider illustration is present in the evidence to corroborate the negotiated platform rate of 0.19%, the fund charges, or any projected values for the recommended plan.
      - The report claims a like-for-like saving of 0.59% on the True Potential switch (existing 0.98% vs new 0.39%), but the existing arrangements table shows True Potential platform charge as 0.40% and the switch table uses 0.98% (which includes the 0.50% ongoing advice charge), making the headline saving figure depend on whether ongoing advice is included on one side and not the other.
      - The Quilter PSO (EA1, £260,000) is absent from the Selectapension switch charges analysis, so no like-for-like charges comparison for the largest single arrangement is present in the evidence.
      - The report's description of the Aviva platform charge as 0.21% vs True Potential 0.40% giving a saving of 0.19% or £88.68: 0.19% × £46,672.80 = £88.68 (arithmetically correct), but 0.21% × £46,672.80 = £97.81, implying the saving calculation uses 0.19% not the difference between 0.21% and 0.40% (0.19%) — this is consistent only if the saving is 0.19 percentage points, which is the difference, so arithmetic is internally consistent, but the Aviva charge used (0.21%) differs from the 0.19% used elsewhere in the same section.
    This requirement assesses whether the benefit-versus-cost claims in the report are consistent with and supported by the evidence. Several material issues arise.
    
    1. INCONSISTENT AVIVA PLATFORM CHARGE RATE: The report quotes the Aviva platform charge as 0.19% (when comparing against Quilter, saving £208 p.a.) and as 0.21% (when comparing against True Potential, saving £88.68). These are two different figures applied to the same recommended platform in the same section of the same report, with no explanation. The SAP analysis (P11) states the Aviva AMC as 0.25% reduced by 0.08%, implying 0.17% — matching neither figure. This is a data inconsistency that undermines the credibility of the cost-saving claims. The guard about different bases applies: the 0.19% may be a negotiated rate applicable to the full combined fund while 0.21% may be a different tier or a pre-negotiation rate, but the report never states this and neither does any evidence document.
    
    2. INITIAL ADVICE FEE BASIS UNDISCLOSED: The report states the initial advice fee is 3% of the amount invested = £9,838.36. The stated transfer value (True Potential + Nest only) is £67,945.44, giving 3% = £2,038.36. The fee of £9,838.36 is arithmetically consistent only with 3% of the full combined fund including the Quilter PSO (c.£327,945). The PSO funds are the largest single element. The report never states that the initial fee is calculated on the full combined fund including the PSO receipt. This is a disclosure shortfall: the client cannot verify the fee from the information given, and the basis for the largest cost item is opaque.
    
    3. ONGOING FEE BASIS: The ongoing fee of circa £3,181.08 at 1% is similarly consistent only with the full combined fund net of the initial fee. Again the basis is not stated. This is a lesser concern as the figure is broadly derivable, but it is not transparent.
    
    4. QUILTER PSO ABSENT FROM SWITCH ANALYSIS: The Quilter PSO (£260,000, the largest arrangement) is absent from the Selectapension switch charges analyses. No like-for-like charges comparison for this arrangement exists in the evidence. The report claims a cost saving of £208 p.a. on the Quilter platform charge (0.27% vs 0.19%), but this claim is unsupported by any corroborating document in the file. No Quilter KFD, illustration or charges schedule is present. The saving is asserted by the report alone, which is the assertion side, not the evidence side.
    
    5. NO AVIVA PROVIDER ILLUSTRATION: The SAP analyses are research/comparison tools. No formal Aviva provider illustration is present in the evidence confirming the negotiated rates, fund charges or projected values for the recommended plan. The SAP (P11) uses 0.25% AMC (−0.08% = 0.17% net), which does not match either the 0.19% or 0.21% quoted in the report. This means the key cost figures in the report have no independent corroboration.
    
    6. LIKE-FOR-LIKE COMPARISON ASYMMETRY: The switch analysis compares existing True Potential at 0.98% (including 0.50% ongoing advice charge) against new plan at 0.39% (excluding ongoing advice). The saving of 0.59% therefore partly reflects removal of the ongoing advice charge rather than a genuine product cost reduction. The report does acknowledge the like-for-like exclusion of ongoing advice in the charges table, but the presentation of the headline saving without making this adjustment explicit in the narrative is a disclosure shortfall.
    
    7. NEST COST INCREASE: The report correctly acknowledges that Nest costs increase by £19.15 p.a. (0.09% on £21,272.64) and provides a benefits rationale. The derived figures confirm this arithmetic. This element is adequately handled.
    
    8. VULNERABILITY: No specific vulnerability characteristics are identified in the evidence that would materially alter this assessment beyond the general need for clear disclosure.
    
    Overall: Multiple material discrepancies exist between the report's cost and benefit claims and the available evidence. The initial fee basis is arithmetically irreconcilable with the stated transfer value and is never explained. The platform charge rate is stated inconsistently within the same document. The largest arrangement (Quilter PSO, £260,000) has no corroborating charges documentation. These are not rounding differences; they go to the heart of whether the client can verify what they are paying and on what basis.

  [G7.6] Costs are excessive relative to the benefits provided
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report states that for the Nest plan, the proposed arrangement will need to outperform the current plan by 1.23% per annum to age 89 (including adviser fees, at 2.94% growth). The initial advice fee is stated as 3% / £9,838.36 and the ongoing adviser charge as 1% per annum / circa £3,181.08 for the first year. The total new ongoing charge is 0.39% per annum (NP1). The like-for-like saving versus True Potential is 0.59% and versus Nest is 0.09%. Benefits cited include consolidation, ongoing servicing, cashflow modelling, and access to the Aviva platform.
    File:     This requirement is assessed from the report against itself and against derived figures. No external corroborating passages were searched for or retrieved. The derived figures show: (1) The required outperformance of 1.23% at 0.09% implies a fund value of £1,556.91, which matches no arrangement value and neither total — the arithmetic basis is not reconcilable from the stated figures. (2) The 0.09% charge difference between EA3 (Nest at 0.30%) and NP1 (0.39%) is confirmed by derived figures. (3) The initial advice fee of £9,838.36 is irreconcilable with 3% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 (which gives £2,038.36); it is only reconcilable with 3% of the full combined fund including the PSO (approximately £327,945), but that basis is never explicitly stated. (4) The ongoing charge monetary equivalent of £3,181.08 is broadly consistent with 1% of the combined fund net of the initial fee, but again the basis is not explicitly stated.
    Discrepancies:
      - The required outperformance of 1.23% per annum for Nest is stated as being based on the difference in charges including adviser fees relative to the current Nest plan. The charge difference between Nest (0.30%) and NP1 (0.39%) is only 0.09%. Even including the 1% ongoing adviser charge, the total new cost attributable to the Nest portion cannot simply be expressed as 1.23% outperformance without a clear basis — the derived figure check shows 1.23% at 0.09% implies a fund of only £1,556.91, which matches no arrangement.
      - The initial advice fee is stated as 3% and £9,838.36, but 3% of the stated transfer value (£67,945.44) equals £2,038.36, not £9,838.36. The report does not state that the fee is calculated on the combined fund including PSO (c.£327,945), so the fee basis is unexplained.
      - The ongoing charge monetary equivalent of £3,181.08 is not reconcilable with 1% of £67,945.44 (= £679.45). It is consistent with 1% of the combined fund including PSO net of the initial fee, but the report does not state this basis explicitly.
      - The report applies the required outperformance test only to Nest, not to True Potential, even though both are being transferred. No equivalent critical-yield or required outperformance figure is given for the True Potential transfer.
      - The Quilter PSO arrangement is excluded from the switch charges assessment table, so no like-for-like cost comparison is presented for the largest single component (£260,000) of the combined fund on which the fees appear to be calculated.
    The report's 1.23% required outperformance figure for Nest sits at the centre of the value assessment for that arrangement. The derived figures reveal that 1.23% at the stated 0.09% charge difference implies a fund of only £1,556.91, which is arithmetically impossible against Nest's current value of £21,272.64. The 0.09% differential is the like-for-like charge saving; the 1.23% must therefore be calculated on a different basis — presumably it reflects the cost of adviser charges applied to the Nest fund value over the projection period. However, the report does not set out how it arrives at 1.23% and the arithmetic cannot be reconstructed from the stated figures. This is not merely an extraction gap: the report quotes the figure without explanation, and the discrepancy between 0.09% and 1.23% is material and unexplained within the document itself.
    
    The initial fee discrepancy is similarly unresolved. The report states 3% and £9,838.36 in the same sentence. 3% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 = £2,038.36. The fee is arithmetically consistent only if calculated on the full combined fund of approximately £327,945 (including the PSO), but the report never states that basis. The PSO is a pension sharing order receipt, which the report itself treats differently from the voluntary switches. This creates a material opacity: the client cannot tell from the report what asset base the initial fee was levied on.
    
    The ongoing monetary figure of £3,181.08 is broadly consistent (within rounding) with 1% of the combined fund net of the initial fee, which supports the interpretation that the fee is on the full c.£327,945. But 'broadly consistent' is not 'explicitly stated', and consistency discovered by back-calculation is not the same as disclosure.
    
    The value justification guard requires testing whether the stated benefits justify the cost before concluding that costs are excessive. The report does describe consolidation benefits, ongoing servicing, cashflow modelling and platform access. For True Potential the like-for-like saving of 0.59% (excluding adviser charge) is a genuine positive. For Nest, however, the recommended arrangement costs 0.09% more on a like-for-like basis, and the 1.23% required outperformance (the figure used to test whether benefits justify that extra cost) cannot be verified arithmetically from the report's own stated figures. That is a compliance concern and a weak justification — the report uses a figure it does not explain to underpin the key value-for-money test for the Nest switch.
    
    No vulnerability flags in the pack appear to affect this specific charge-disclosure finding beyond general good-disclosure obligations.
    
    The Quilter PSO is the largest single component of the combined fund, yet it is absent from the switch charges comparison table. If the initial fee and ongoing charge are indeed levied on the combined fund (as back-calculation suggests), the absence of a cost-benefit disclosure for the PSO component is a material omission.
    
    The guard ('a higher cost is not automatically poor value') is noted: the report does provide benefit statements. But those benefit statements are used to justify a 1.23% required outperformance figure that the report does not explain, applied only to Nest and not to True Potential, and supported by fee arithmetic that does not reconcile with the stated transfer value. The guard does not explain away those specific discrepancies.

  [G7.7] Alternatives provide better cost or benefit to the client
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The canonical model holds no extracted value at any of the comparativeCost paths for alternativesConsidered. The report itself (passages [P10], [P9]) asserts: (1) Quilter switch — negotiated platform charge of 0.19% vs Quilter 0.27%, saving £208 p.a.; (2) True Potential and Nest switch — like-for-like ongoing charge saving of 0.59% p.a. (existing 0.98% vs new 0.39%); (3) Aviva offers lower overall cost structure, fund range, active monitoring, and CIP governance as benefits. The report states options 1–5 were considered and option 5 (new Aviva plan) was selected.
    File:     Three SelectaPension switching analyses are present in the file: (P1/P2) 'All charges inc existing OAC' — at 5.88% growth, existing schemes project £179,355 (TP) plus Nest equivalent, Aviva PCLS £26,600 vs existing £30,400, meaning the existing schemes produce a higher projected lump sum on this scenario; (P3/P4) 'All charges ex existing OAC' — Aviva PCLS £36,600 vs existing £33,600, showing Aviva higher; (P5/P6) 'IAC only ex existing OAC' — Aviva PCLS £36,600 vs existing £33,600; (P7/P8) 'No charges ex existing OAC' — Aviva projected fund at 2.94% £151,000 vs existing £134,612, RIY 0.4%. The switching analyses cover only True Potential and Nest (transfer value £67,945.44) and do not include the Quilter PSO (£260,000). No SelectaPension analysis comparing Aviva against Quilter on a standalone basis is present. The 'inc existing OAC' scenario (P2) shows the existing schemes producing a higher PCLS (£30,400 vs £26,600) where the ongoing adviser charge is included on the existing side — i.e. when both sides bear the adviser charge, the existing arrangement projects better at retirement. The 'ex existing OAC' and 'IAC only' scenarios show Aviva marginally better. The critical yield in the no-charges scenario (P14) is 0.09% above the assumed growth rate, meaning Aviva needs only a tiny outperformance.
    Discrepancies:
      - The 'All charges inc existing OAC' scenario (P2) shows the existing schemes produce a higher projected PCLS at retirement (£30,400) than the Aviva recommendation (£26,600) — the report does not disclose or address this scenario.
      - The switching analyses in the file cover only True Potential and Nest (£67,945.44). No comparative cost analysis covering the Quilter PSO (£260,000 — the largest single asset) against alternatives is present in the file, yet the report asserts the Aviva platform is cheaper than Quilter.
      - The canonical model has no value at /recommendations[]/alternativesConsidered[]/comparativeCost — the comparative cost basis underpinning the recommendation is not captured in the extracted model.
      - The report's claim of a £208 p.a. saving on the Quilter transfer relies on 0.19% vs 0.27% platform charge, but no switching analysis or provider illustration for the Quilter PSO is present to support this figure independently.
      - Options 1–4 are listed as considered but no cost figures or projected outcomes for those options appear in the report or the file, so the comparative cost basis for rejecting them is absent.
    The requirement is that the report demonstrates the recommended arrangement offers better cost or benefit than available alternatives, supported by consistent evidence. Three issues arise after applying the guards.
    
    1. The 'inc existing OAC' scenario (P2): When the existing ongoing adviser charge is included on both sides of the comparison, the existing True Potential and Nest arrangements project a higher PCLS at retirement (£30,400 vs £26,600 for Aviva). The guard permits a cheaper option to be rejected for non-cost reasons, but that guard applies where the report acknowledges the cost difference and explains why benefits justify it. Here the report does not disclose this scenario at all — it relies on the 'ex existing OAC' scenario to assert a cost saving, without explaining that including the adviser charge on the existing side reverses the outcome. This is a disclosure shortfall: the report presents only the scenario favourable to the recommendation.
    
    2. Quilter PSO (£260,000) — the largest component: The report asserts a saving of £208 p.a. based on 0.19% vs 0.27%, but no switching analysis, illustration or independent charge schedule for the Quilter plan is in the file. The charge figures are self-reported. No comparative projection for the Quilter funds vs Aviva exists in the evidence. The claim is therefore unsupported by external evidence — this is missing evidence, not an extraction failure, because the extraction self-report does not flag the absence of a Quilter analysis as a pipeline defect.
    
    3. Options 1–4: The report lists five options and selects option 5, but no cost or projected outcome data for options 1–4 is present. Without those figures, the comparative cost case for option 5 over the alternatives is a bare assertion.
    
    The guard that a cheaper option rejected for non-cost reasons is valid does not assist here because: (a) on one scenario the recommended option is not the cheaper one; and (b) for the Quilter PSO, the comparative cost evidence is simply absent. The vulnerability overlay: no vulnerability characteristics in the file are identified as materially affecting this cost comparison assessment specifically (CHK-010 covers vulnerability in its own right). Severity is moderate: the 'inc OAC' scenario reversal is a disclosure shortfall that a reviewer cannot overlook, and the missing Quilter analysis covers the majority of the fund value, but the overall recommendation is not arithmetically impossible and the other scenarios do show a cost saving on the TP/Nest portion.

  [G7.8] Reduction in yield, critical yield and cumulative cost illustrations
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The suitability report states three RIY/critical yield scenarios for the recommended Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice): (1) Low/0% growth — RIY 1.5%, critical yield 0.41% (+0.41%), effect of switching -12.42%; (2) Mid/2.94% growth — RIY 1.5%, critical yield 3.37% (+0.43%), effect of switching -12.42%; (3) High/5.88% growth — RIY 1.6%, critical yield 6.32% (+0.44%), effect of switching -12.42%. These figures are attributed to a section headed 'Critical Yield' in the suitability report.
    File:     Passage [P7] (Annes M SAP All charges inc existing OAC 24.3.26.md) contains a results summary table showing exactly the figures the report asserts: RIY 1.5% at 0% and 2.94%, RIY 1.6% at 5.88%, critical yields 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%), 6.32% (+0.44%), and effect of switching -12.42% across all three growth rates. Passage [P6] also from the same document corroborates RIY of 1.5%. This is the 'All charges including existing OAC' scenario. However, three other switch analysis documents are on file showing materially different figures: [P2] (no charges ex existing OAC) shows RIY 1%, 1%, 1.1% and effect of switching +20.85%; [P4] (IAC only ex existing OAC) shows RIY 0.5% and effect of switching producing higher projected values for Aviva; [P10] (no charges ex existing OAC, Nest) shows RIY 0.4%, 0.4%, 0.4% and effect of -2.68%.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report quotes RIY figures (1.5%, 1.5%, 1.6%) that match only the 'All charges including existing OAC' illustration [P7], not the 'IAC only ex existing OAC' [P4] or 'no charges ex existing OAC' [P2/P10] scenarios which show different RIY values (0.5%, 1.0%/1.1%, 0.4% respectively). Multiple scenarios are on file and the report does not identify which scenario underpins its quoted figures.
      - The 'All charges inc existing OAC' scenario in [P7] includes both ongoing adviser charges on the existing plan and Aviva charges; the report does not state this basis explicitly, creating a disclosure shortfall on what cost basis was used.
      - [P2] shows the effect of switching as +20.85% (positive — a benefit to the client) under the 'no charges ex existing OAC' scenario, directly contrasting with the -12.42% in the report; the different scenarios produce qualitatively opposite conclusions about whether switching benefits the client.
      - [P4] (IAC only ex existing OAC) shows Aviva projected values higher than the existing plan (e.g. £58,200 vs £53,497.38 at 0% growth and RIY 0.5%), whereas [P7] (all charges inc existing OAC) shows Aviva lower (£42,300 vs £48,408.31 at 0%). The report quotes only the latter scenario without explaining the choice or acknowledging the contrast.
      - The report does not identify retirement age 87 as the projection basis, yet all switch analysis documents use age 87. This is not a contradiction but is a missing disclosure.
      - The figures in [P3] show a lump sum at retirement comparison of £33,600 (existing) vs £26,600 (Aviva) under the 'all charges' scenario, consistent with a negative switching effect, but this comparison is not referenced in the report's costs and charges section.
    The RIY, critical yield and effect-of-switching figures quoted in the report (1.5%, 0.41%, -12.42% etc.) are confirmed verbatim in passage [P7], which is the 'All charges including existing OAC' switch analysis dated 24 March 2026. The arithmetic check corroborates this: 1.5% in [P6], 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%), 6.32% (+0.44%), and -12.42% in [P7] all match the report's assertions. So the figures the report quotes are internally consistent with one specific document on file.
    
    However, there are three additional switch analysis scenarios on file ([P2] no charges ex existing OAC, [P4] IAC only ex existing OAC, [P10] no charges ex existing OAC for Nest) that show materially different outcomes. In particular, under the 'no charges' scenario [P2], the effect of switching is +20.85% (positive) rather than -12.42%. Under the IAC-only scenario [P4], Aviva projected values exceed the existing plan at all growth rates, showing a benefit rather than a cost. The report quotes only the most adverse scenario without disclosing which scenario has been used or why, and without explaining to the client that under a like-for-like (ex-OAC) comparison the switch produces a positive outcome.
    
    The critical distinction is the treatment of the ongoing adviser charge (OAC) on the existing plan. The 'all charges inc existing OAC' scenario charges the existing plan with the current 0.5% ongoing adviser fee, which artificially reduces the existing plan's projected value and makes the Aviva outcome look worse by comparison. Using this scenario to justify the switch is a different exercise from a like-for-like product cost comparison. The report does not explain this basis to the client, which is a material disclosure shortfall.
    
    The guard regarding growth rate basis is satisfied — all figures being compared use the same three growth rates (0%, 2.94%, 5.88%). The guard on retirement age is not a concern since all scenarios use age 87. No vulnerability factors in the file bear specifically on this arithmetic finding.
    
    The core issue is not that the figures are wrong, but that the report presents one scenario's RIY and critical yield figures as the definitive answer without disclosing: (a) that multiple scenarios exist; (b) which charging basis was used; and (c) that under a like-for-like product cost scenario the switch produces a positive outcome. This is a disclosure shortfall and a data quality concern about the basis of the illustration used.

  [G7.9] The service proposition attached to the ongoing adviser charge
  Potential Concern — severity Low — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report charges 1% per annum for an ongoing service comprising: (1) reviewing attitude to risk and capacity for loss to ensure the portfolio remains suitable; (2) reviewing the ongoing suitability of the investment portfolio; (3) market updates and updates on changes in financial legislation; (4) access to a professional, experienced and qualified team of financial advisers at any time for ad-hoc advice; (5) access to full holistic financial planning. No review frequency is specified. The monetary equivalent is stated as circa £3,181.08 for the first year.
    File:     The client agreement (TFAS Wealth Client Agreement) states: 'Ongoing Services - For most investment funds that we transact on your behalf, we charge an ongoing annual review management charge of 1% (subject to a minimum fee of £100), which is automatically taken from the funds invested.' The agreement also references 'Providing periodic on-going reviews of your existing and/or any new investments we arrange on your behalf to ensure they remain suitable for you.' The broader services document referenced ('Important Information about our Services') is not in the evidence pack. The client agreement does not itemise the five specific services listed in the report; it describes the service generically as an ongoing annual review management charge. The agreement confirms the 1% charge. No review frequency is specified in the agreement either.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report lists five specific service components (risk review, suitability review, market/legislation updates, ad-hoc adviser access, holistic financial planning) but the client agreement describes only a generic 'ongoing annual review management charge' without itemising these components.
      - The report does not specify a review frequency; the client agreement is similarly silent on frequency beyond 'periodic'.
      - The 'Important Information about our Services' document referenced in the client agreement as containing details of the ongoing service is not in the evidence pack, so the fuller service description that document may contain cannot be verified.
      - No review frequency is stated in either the report or the client agreement.
    The 1% ongoing charge is consistent between the report and the client agreement — both state 1% per annum deducted from the fund. The client agreement corroborates the charge level. However, the client agreement describes the service only generically as an 'ongoing annual review management charge', while the report enumerates five distinct service components. The agreement cross-refers to a separate 'Important Information about our Services' document for the full service description, but that document is not in the evidence pack. This means the five itemised services in the report cannot be fully corroborated by file evidence beyond the generic description in the agreement. This is a disclosure shortfall / missing evidence concern rather than a contradiction: there is no conflict between the report and the agreement, but the more detailed service proposition in the report lacks independent corroboration from the referenced document. The absence of any review frequency in both the report and the agreement is a gap — clients paying 1% p.a. should understand how often they will be reviewed. This is a modest but real disclosure shortfall. No vulnerability indicators in the file appear to heighten concern on this specific point beyond the general level.

  [G7.10] Benefits relied upon to justify a higher cost
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report asserts multiple cost-benefit claims: (1) Quilter transfer: Aviva platform charge 0.19% vs Quilter 0.27%, saving £208 p.a.; (2) True Potential transfer: Aviva platform charge 0.21% vs TP 0.40%, saving 0.19% or £88.68; fund charges 0.20% vs 0.58%, saving £177.35 p.a.; like-for-like saving of 0.59% (£275.37 p.a. underperformance tolerance); critical yield: proposed can underperform by 0.01% p.a. to age 89; (3) Nest transfer: Aviva is 0.09% MORE expensive than Nest, costing £19.15 p.a. more; critical yield: proposed must outperform by 1.23% p.a. to age 89 at 2.94% growth; (4) Benefits stated: investment flexibility, online platform access, flexible retirement options, ongoing monitoring. For the True Potential comparison, the report states Aviva platform charge is 0.21%. For the Quilter comparison, the report states the £208 saving is based on estimated fund value and 0.19% vs 0.27%. The ongoing adviser charge is stated as 1% p.a. (circa £3,181.08 first year). The initial advice fee is stated as 3% = £9,838.36.
    File:     The switch analysis documents (P1, P4) confirm True Potential fund value £46,672.80 and Nest personal pension details. P3 shows Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) projected values at 0% / 2.94% / 5.88% growth rates outperforming the existing plan on inflation-adjusted basis (RIY 0.4%). The Aviva KFD (P6, P10) confirms flexible drawdown options. The Vanguard/HSBC KIIDs (P11, P12) show the recommended funds. P11 shows a 0.19% ongoing charge for one of the recommended funds. The switch analysis documents include IAC-only and all-charges variants. The derived figures confirm: Aviva platform charge of 0.19% implies fund value of £327,947 (all arrangements combined); Aviva fund charge of 0.20% implies fund value of £327,945 (all arrangements). EA2 total ongoing 0.98% is arithmetically consistent with True Potential value of £46,672.80. EA3 total ongoing 0.30% is arithmetically consistent with Nest value of £21,272.64. NP1 total ongoing 0.39% is arithmetically consistent with total of all arrangements £327,946. The critical yield figure of 1.23% for Nest implies a fund value of £1,556.91 — which matches no arrangement value or total. The report states Aviva platform charge for True Potential comparison is 0.21%, but the derived figure for the Aviva platform charge (0.19% giving £623.10) implies the negotiated rate used for Quilter is 0.19% applied to the full £327,947. No switch analysis document in the pack covers the Quilter PSO comparison. There is no evidence in the file corroborating the £208 Quilter saving, the 0.27% Quilter charge, or the £88.68 True Potential platform saving figure.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report states the Aviva platform charge for the True Potential comparison is 0.21%, but the derived figures show the Aviva platform charge used elsewhere (Quilter comparison and NP1 total) is 0.19%. Two different Aviva platform charge rates are used in the same recommendation without explanation.
      - The report states the Quilter platform charge is 0.27% and the saving is £208 p.a. No switch analysis or provider document in the case file corroborates these figures. The Quilter PSO is absent from the switch charges analysis documents.
      - The critical yield of 1.23% for the Nest comparison implies a fund value of £1,556.91 (per derived figures), which matches no arrangement value or total in the case. This figure cannot be arithmetically reconciled with the Nest current value of £21,272.64 or any other stated value.
      - The report states the True Potential like-for-like saving is 0.59% / £275.37 p.a. underperformance tolerance, but the internalInconsistencies note that 0.98% existing vs 0.39% new = 0.59% difference; however the 0.98% includes the 0.50% ongoing advice charge, making the like-for-like basis unclear — the report excludes ongoing advice but includes it in the 0.98% figure creating an inconsistent comparison basis.
      - The ongoing service charge monetary equivalent of £3,181.08 p.a. is broadly reconcilable only if the PSO funds of ~£260,000 are included in the base, but this combined total is never explicitly stated as the basis, leaving the figure without a transparent derivation in the report.
      - The initial advice fee of £9,838.36 stated as 3% of amount invested cannot be reconciled with 3% of the stated transfer value of £67,945.44 (which gives £2,038.36). The basis including PSO funds (~£327,945) is never explicitly stated.
      - No switch analysis covering the Quilter PSO is present in the case file, so the cost-benefit justification for that transfer (£208 saving, 0.19% vs 0.27%) rests solely on the report's own assertion with no independent corroboration.
    The report makes several cost-benefit claims that require scrutiny under G7.10. Three issues stand out as material findings after applying the guards.
    
    1. AVIVA PLATFORM CHARGE INCONSISTENCY: The report uses 0.21% as the Aviva platform charge when comparing against True Potential, but 0.19% when comparing against Quilter and as the negotiated rate in the charges table. The derived figures confirm 0.19% is applied to the full combined fund. This is an internal inconsistency within the report — two different rates for the same Aviva platform charge in the same recommendation document — which is not explained by any guard (the guard about different bases applies where two documents use different bases, but here the report itself uses two different figures). The 0.21% vs 0.40% comparison for True Potential and the resulting £88.68 saving may therefore be overstated if the true rate is 0.19%.
    
    2. QUILTER SWITCH — NO CORROBORATING EVIDENCE: The claim that Aviva is cheaper than Quilter by £208 p.a. (0.19% vs 0.27%) is made only in the report [P24, I category]. No switch analysis, Quilter provider document, or other non-report source in the case file corroborates the 0.27% Quilter charge or the £208 saving. Per the instruction, a claim supported only by [I] passages has no support in the case file. This is the most significant evidence gap given that the Quilter PSO transfer involves approximately £260,000 — the largest single transfer. The benefits listed for the Quilter transfer (lower cost, monitoring, flexibility) are stated but unverified against any independent document in the pack.
    
    3. CRITICAL YIELD FOR NEST (1.23%) — ARITHMETIC INCONSISTENCY: The derived figures show that 1.23% applied to the required outperformance of £19.15 p.a. implies a fund of only £1,556.91 — which matches no arrangement. The Nest fund value is £21,272.64. If the critical yield were applied to £21,272.64, 1.23% would imply an annual shortfall of ~£261.65, not £19.15. The £19.15 figure is the annual cost difference at 0.09% of £21,272.64 (arithmetically consistent). But the critical yield of 1.23% applied to this base produces ~£261.65 annual required outperformance, not £19.15. The 1.23% figure therefore appears to have been computed on a different (much smaller) base, and is not reconcilable with the Nest fund value. This is a data inconsistency that could mislead the client about the hurdle rate they face.
    
    4. TRUE POTENTIAL LIKE-FOR-LIKE BASIS: The 0.98% existing charge for True Potential includes 0.50% ongoing advice charge per the internalInconsistencies note. Excluding advice from both sides to get like-for-like: existing = 0.48%, new = 0.39%, difference = 0.09% — not 0.59%. The 0.59% saving uses existing 0.98% vs new 0.39% — i.e. it excludes advice on the new side but includes it on the existing side. The report acknowledges it excludes ongoing advice, but the 0.98% figure it uses apparently includes it. This is an inconsistent comparison basis that inflates the stated saving from 0.09% to 0.59%.
    
    Vulnerability overlay: No specific vulnerability characteristics are identified in the evidence that would materially change this assessment, though the arithmetic inconsistencies noted above are particularly concerning where clients may be relying on clear cost disclosure to make an informed decision.
    
    The guards considered: consolidation convenience vs financial benefit — the report does not rely solely on consolidation; it relies on specific charge percentages and monetary savings which are the subject of the discrepancies. Benefits the existing plans already provide — flexible drawdown and online access are generic to both platforms, but the report's primary justification rests on cost savings, not these ancillary benefits. The charge basis guard — the True Potential 0.98% vs 0.39% comparison uses different bases (one including advice charge, one excluding) making the comparison invalid on its own terms.

  [G7.11] Higher charges producing lower projected outcomes or an increased yield requirement
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The report states that for the Nest comparison (including adviser fees) the Aviva plan is more expensive and the proposed plan must outperform the current Nest plan by 1.23% per annum to age 89 at 2.94% growth. For the True Potential comparison, the Aviva plan is also more expensive but can underperform by only 0.01% pa to age 89. A Critical Yield table (retirement age 87) shows Aviva projected values of £42,300 / £106,000 / £261,000 at 0% / 2.94% / 5.88% growth respectively, with RIY of 1.5% (low/mid) and 1.6% (high), and rates of return required from Aviva of 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%) and 6.32% (+0.44%). The required outperformance of 1.23% is stated to be 'pa to age 89 (including adviser fees, at 2.94% growth) for Nest'.
    File:     [P4] (Annes M SAP All charges inc existing OAC) provides the combined critical yield table covering both True Potential and Nest together: Existing Schemes at £48,408 / £121,807 / £298,632; Aviva at £42,300 / £106,000 / £261,000; rates of return required 0.41% (+0.41%), 3.37% (+0.43%), 6.32% (+0.44%); fund effect -12.42%; RIY 1.5% / 1.5% / 1.6%. [P3] corroborates the Aviva projected values and 1.5% RIY. [P17] (the suitability report itself) states the 1.23% outperformance figure and the 0.01% underperformance figure for True Potential. [P14] (Annes M SAP no charges ex existing OAC, Nest only, no adviser charge scenario) shows Nest standalone: Existing £19,334 / £48,651 / £119,277; Aviva £18,800 / £47,300 / £116,000; RIY 0.3% (existing) and 0.4% (proposed); rate of return required 0.09% (+0.09%) at 0%, 3.03% (+0.09%) at 2.94%, 5.97% (+0.09%) at 5.88%; fund effect -2.68%. [P6] (no charges ex existing OAC, combined) shows higher Aviva projected values (£60,100 / £151,000 / £370,000) with the existing at £53,497 / £134,612 / £330,027, producing positive fund effect +12.34% and negative critical yield differentials (-0.36%, -0.38%, -0.39%), i.e. Aviva outperforms in that scenario. The 1.23% required outperformance figure is arithmetically corroborated in [P2] (value 1.23 appears). The critical yield arithmetic: derived figures confirm the combined fund base used for Aviva platform and investment charges is ~£327,945 (all three arrangements), whereas the SAP tools use only £67,945 (True Potential + Nest transfers, excluding the Quilter PSO). The SAP tool labelled 'All charges inc existing OAC' uses the combined True Potential + Nest base (£67,945) and produces the projected values cited in the report. No SAP output covering the Nest arrangement in isolation with adviser charges included is presented in the evidence to directly derive the 1.23% figure; that figure appears only in the report itself ([P17]) and is numerically matched in [P2] within a performance table context.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report's Critical Yield table uses retirement age 87 for the projected values (£42,300/£106,000/£261,000) but the required outperformance narrative states age 89 as the term for the 1.23% and 0.01% figures. The SAP output in [P4] also uses age 87. The basis for the age-89 outperformance figures is not evidenced by any SAP document in the file.
      - The 1.23% required outperformance appears in [P17] (the report itself) and is numerically matched in [P2], but [P2] is a performance table row (monthly return for Jan '26 = 1.23), not a critical yield output. No standalone SAP analysis for Nest with adviser charges included and retirement age 89 is present in the evidence pack to corroborate that specific figure independently.
      - The SAP output 'All charges inc existing OAC' ([P3]/[P4]) covers True Potential + Nest combined and shows Aviva produces lower projected values at all growth rates (£42,300 vs £48,408 at 0%; £106,000 vs £121,807 at 2.94%; £261,000 vs £298,632 at 5.88%), with -12.42% immediate fund effect. The report discloses higher cost but does not provide a standalone Nest-only analysis (with adviser charges, age 89) in the supporting documents to independently verify the 1.23% figure.
      - The report states the required outperformance for True Potential (including adviser fees) is only 0.01% underperformance. This figure is not matched by any SAP output in the pack — [P4] shows a combined True Potential + Nest analysis, not a True Potential standalone analysis with adviser charges included.
      - The projected values in the report (£42,300/£106,000/£261,000) and [P3]/[P4] are for the combined True Potential + Nest transfer (£67,945) scenario rather than reflecting the full combined fund including the Quilter PSO (~£327,945). The derived figures confirm that the Aviva platform charge (0.19%) and investment charge (0.20%) are computed on ~£327,945, meaning ongoing charges in reality apply to a much larger base than the SAP tool appears to use.
      - The RIY of 1.5% stated in the report and [P3]/[P4] applies to the combined True Potential + Nest base (~£67,945), but the ongoing adviser charge of 1% and platform charges are applied to the full ~£327,945. This means the RIY as presented materially understates the cost impact on the total funds under management.
    The report correctly identifies that the Aviva recommendation is more expensive than both existing arrangements when adviser charges are included and discloses the required outperformance figures (1.23% for Nest; 0.01% underperformance tolerance for True Potential). The Critical Yield table in the report and [P3]/[P4] are consistent for the Aviva projected values and the combined critical yield metrics.
    
    However, two material concerns arise:
    
    1. Age mismatch: The projected values table (£42,300/£106,000/£261,000) uses retirement age 87, consistent with [P4]. But the narrative outperformance figures (1.23% and 0.01%) are stated to be 'to age 89'. No SAP output in the evidence pack runs to age 89, so the 1.23% figure cannot be independently corroborated from the supporting documents — it appears only in the report itself ([P17]) and a numeric coincidence in a performance table ([P2]). This is an unsupported assertion about a material cost impact figure.
    
    2. Incomplete scope of the analysis: The derived figures establish that the Aviva platform and fund charges are applied to the full ~£327,945 (all three arrangements including the Quilter PSO), yet the SAP critical yield tools in the pack cover only the True Potential + Nest transfer of ~£67,945. The Quilter PSO (£260,000) is the largest component of the portfolio but is not included in the switch charges assessment or the critical yield analysis. The RIY of 1.5% and the critical yield figures therefore reflect only about 21% of the total fund value. The ongoing charges (1% adviser, 0.19% platform, 0.20% funds = 1.39% total) on the full £327,945 represent approximately £4,558/year, but the SAP analysis is run on £67,945, implying ongoing charges of ~£944/year. This is a material understatement of the cost burden disclosed to the client.
    
    The guard noting that required outperformance under 0.5% is within noise applies to the True Potential figure (0.01%), but 1.23% for Nest is well above that threshold and is a meaningful hurdle that is not independently evidenced.
    
    The vulnerability overlay: no vulnerability characteristics are identified in the evidence pack that would materially alter this assessment beyond the general concern that a client should clearly understand the full cost impact on their total portfolio.
    
    Overall: the report discloses higher cost and provides some analysis, but the key outperformance figure (1.23%, Nest, age 89) lacks independent documentary support, and the critical yield / RIY analysis omits the largest component of the client's funds (Quilter PSO ~£260,000), meaning the cumulative cost impact is materially understated in the documentation presented to the client.

CHK-008 — Disadvantages, Risks and Customer Understanding
Outcome: Potential Concern

  Potential Concern across 8 requirement(s). 8 raised a concern (G8.1, G8.2, G8.3, G8.4, G8.5, G8.6, G8.7, G8.8). The report does not state any tax consequences specific to this pension consolidation — the Aviva KFD [P12] explicitly flags tax implications as a material consideration when transferring, but the report contains only a generic disclaimer that tax treatment is based on current law. No client-specific tax consequence (e.g. annual allowance position, recycling risk, income tax on drawdown) is addressed. No projected retirement value for NP1 is stated in the report (confirmed absent by extraction metadata), meaning no forward-looking loss scenario anchored to the client's retirement age is presented, though critical yield and a -12.42% switching impact figure are given. The -12.42% switching effect appears in the critical yield section; the basis (which arrangement(s) it applies to, what growth assumption it uses) is not made explicit in the report, making it difficult for the client to contextualise the figure. 1 cited quote(s) could not be found in the evidence supplied and were rejected.

  [G8.1] Material disadvantages, risk factors and limitations of the recommendation
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report discloses the following material disadvantages and limitations: (1) Higher charges vs Nest: 0.39% vs 0.30%, an increase of £19.15 p.a. (2) Critical yield vs Nest: Aviva must outperform Nest by 1.23% p.a. to age 89 at 2.94% growth assumption. (3) Critical yield vs True Potential: Aviva can underperform True Potential by 0.01% p.a. to age 89. (4) Switching effect on fund: -12.42% change in maturity value. (5) Market movement risk during transfer. (6) Cancellation restriction — previous provider not obliged to take plan back. (7) Performance not guaranteed. (8) Transaction costs excluded from quoted charges. (9) Charge illustration discrepancy caveat. No projected retirement value (NP1) is stated in the report; the extraction flags this as absent. No explicit tax consequences of the transfer are discussed in the report narrative beyond a generic statement that tax treatment is based on current law and HMRC practice.
    File:     The Aviva KFD [P2, P4, P5, P11, P12] confirms the following risks: value can go down as well as up; client could get back less than invested; transferring pensions involves charges, fund range considerations, valuable benefits that could be lost, and tax implications; if transferring from a defined benefit scheme, guaranteed income could be lost; market movement during transfer; cancellation provisions including that the previous provider need not take the plan back; income drawdown risks including depletion of fund and impact on means-tested benefits. The SAP research files [P17, P18, P19, P22] provide early transfer analysis tables showing estimated transfer values at 0%, 2.94% and 5.88% growth rates from year 1 onwards, confirming quantified loss scenarios exist in the file. The report's critical yield figures and switching effect (-12.42%) originate from the G-category SAP documents. No passage in the file records a tax consequence specific to this client's transfer (e.g. potential recycling, annual allowance, or LTA considerations). No passage identifies the client as a defined benefit member, so the DB-transfer warning in the KFD is not a gap specific to this case. The vulnerability assessment detail is flagged as absent from the canonical model.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report does not state any tax consequences specific to this pension consolidation — the Aviva KFD [P12] explicitly flags tax implications as a material consideration when transferring, but the report contains only a generic disclaimer that tax treatment is based on current law. No client-specific tax consequence (e.g. annual allowance position, recycling risk, income tax on drawdown) is addressed.
      - No projected retirement value for NP1 is stated in the report (confirmed absent by extraction metadata), meaning no forward-looking loss scenario anchored to the client's retirement age is presented, though critical yield and a -12.42% switching impact figure are given.
      - The -12.42% switching effect appears in the critical yield section; the basis (which arrangement(s) it applies to, what growth assumption it uses) is not made explicit in the report, making it difficult for the client to contextualise the figure.
      - The report characterises the 1.23% required outperformance vs Nest as a 'disadvantage' but does not explain whether this is achievable or what the consequences would be if not achieved — no narrative outcome or monetary illustration accompanies this figure.
      - The Aviva KFD [P4] notes that charges can be taken from income or investment and that this affects growth or income, and that insufficient cash leads to forced disinvestment — these product-specific risks are not reflected in the report's risk disclosures.
      - The Aviva KFD [P2] describes income drawdown risks (fund depletion, impact on means-tested benefits, less money for dependants or annuity purchase) — these are absent from the report's disadvantages section, though the recommendation concerns a pension consolidation rather than an immediate drawdown, reducing but not eliminating their materiality.
      - The report does not address whether features or guarantees are being given up in the existing True Potential or Nest arrangements (featuresOrGuaranteesLost flagged as absent from all three replacement analyses in the extraction metadata).
    The report does disclose several material, client-specific disadvantages: the charge differential vs Nest, critical yield analysis for both existing plans, and the -12.42% switching effect. These are quantified and client-specific, satisfying the core requirement for a loss scenario. The SAP research files in category G corroborate the existence of a critical yield analysis and early transfer value tables, which the report draws upon. However, three concerns stand above the threshold for a potential concern. First, tax consequences: the Aviva KFD [P12] expressly states that tax implications are a material consideration on pension transfer, and the report contains nothing client-specific about tax — no mention of annual allowance, no mention of how transferred funds will be taxed on drawdown, no recycling risk. This is a disclosure shortfall against a material item the product literature flags. Second, features and guarantees: the extraction metadata confirms that featuresOrGuaranteesLost is absent from all three replacement analyses. The report does not state whether any protected benefits, guaranteed annuity rates or other features attach to the True Potential or Nest plans, nor does it say they do not. This is a missing component. Third, the -12.42% switching effect lacks a stated basis — which arrangement it applies to and what growth assumption underpins it are not made explicit, weakening the client's ability to understand this figure. The income drawdown risks in the KFD (forced disinvestment, depletion risk, means-tested benefits) are absent from the report, but these are less material given that no drawdown is being set up now. The vulnerability assessment detail is flagged absent from the canonical model; no vulnerability indicators are apparent from the fact find [P1] that would materially alter the disclosure requirement for this check. Overall the report presents a reasonable risk disclosure framework with quantified disadvantages, but falls short on tax consequences and the features/guarantees question.
